then $(e,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}(e))$ appears twice in ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$. However, in the interest of readability we will avoid this more cumbersome multiset
formalism, and abuse notation slightly by calling ${\pi_{1}}@{\pi_{2}}$ a policy. This issue arises
whenever we run some policy and then run another from a fresh start. during the running of $\pi_{1}$.

###### Definition 35 (Adaptive Monotonicity (Alternate Version))

A function $f:2^{E}\times O^{E}\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}$ is *adaptive monotone* with respect to distribution ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{p\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}}\right)}}$ if for all policies $\pi$ and $\pi^{\prime}$,
it holds that$f_{\text{avg}}(\pi)\leq f_{\text{avg}}({\pi^{\prime}}@{\pi})$, where$f_{\text{avg}}(\pi):=\mathbb{E}\left[f(E({\pi},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}})\right]$ is defined w.r.t. ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{p\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}}\right)}}$.

###### Lemma 36 (Adaptive Monotonicity Equivalence)

Fix a function $f:2^{E}\times O^{E}\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}$.
Then $\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)\geq 0$ for all ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$ with $\mathbb{P}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\right]>0$ and all $e\in E$ if and only if
for all policies $\pi$ and $\pi^{\prime}$,$f_{\text{avg}}(\pi)\leq f_{\text{avg}}({\pi^{\prime}}@{\pi})$.

Proof  Fix policies $\pi$ and $\pi^{\prime}$.
We begin by proving $f_{\text{avg}}({\pi^{\prime}}@{\pi})=f_{\text{avg}}({\pi}@{\pi^{\prime}})$. Fix any ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}$ and
note that$E({{\pi^{\prime}}@{\pi}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}})=E({\pi^{\prime}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}})\cup E({\pi},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}})=E({{\pi}@{\pi^{\prime}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}})$.
Hence

|  | $f_{\text{avg}}({\pi^{\prime}}@{\pi})=\mathbb{E}\left[f(E({{\pi^{\prime}}@{\pi}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}})\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[f(E({{\pi}@{\pi^{\prime}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}})\right]=f_{\text{avg}}({\pi}@{\pi^{\prime}}).$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- |

Therefore$f_{\text{avg}}(\pi)\leq f_{\text{avg}}({\pi^{\prime}}@{\pi})$ holds if and
only if $f_{\text{avg}}(\pi)\leq f_{\text{avg}}({\pi}@{\pi^{\prime}})$.

We first prove the forward direction.
Suppose $\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)\geq 0$ for all ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$ and all $e\in E$.
Note the expression $f_{\text{avg}}({\pi}@{\pi^{\prime}})-f_{\text{avg}}(\pi)$ can be written as a conical combination of (nonnegative) $\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)$ terms, i.e.,
for some $\alpha\geq\mathbf{0}$, $f_{\text{avg}}({\pi}@{\pi^{\prime}})-f_{\text{avg}}(\pi)=\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},e}\alpha_{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},e)}\,\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)$.
Hence $f_{\text{avg}}({\pi}@{\pi^{\prime}})-f_{\text{avg}}(\pi)\geq 0$ and so $f_{\text{avg}}(\pi)\leq f_{\text{avg}}({\pi}@{\pi^{\prime}})=f_{\text{avg}}({\pi^{\prime}}@{\pi})$.

We next prove the backward direction, in contrapositive form.
Suppose $\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)<0$ for some ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$ with $\mathbb{P}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\right]>0$ and $e\in E$.
Let $e_{1},\ldots,e_{r}$ be the items in $\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})$ and define policies$\pi$ and $\pi^{\prime}$ as follows. For $i=1,2,\ldots,r$, both$\pi$ and $\pi^{\prime}$ select $e_{i}$ and observe ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}(e_{i})$.
If either policy observes ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}(e_{i})\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}(e_{i})$ it immediately terminates, otherwise it continues.
If $\pi$ succeeds in selecting all of $\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})$ then it terminates.
If $\pi^{\prime}$ succeeds in selecting all of $\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})$ then it selects $e$ and then terminates.
We claim $f_{\text{avg}}({\pi}@{\pi^{\prime}})-f_{\text{avg}}(\pi)<0$.
Note that$E({{\pi}@{\pi^{\prime}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}})=E({\pi},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}})$ unless ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$, and if${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$ then$E({{\pi}@{\pi^{\prime}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}})=E({\pi},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}})\cup\left\{e\right\}$ and also$E({\pi},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}})=\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})$.
Hence

|  | $\displaystyle f_{\text{avg}}({\pi}@{\pi^{\prime}})-f_{\text{avg}}(\pi)$ | $\displaystyle=$ | $\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[f(E({{\pi}@{\pi^{\prime}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}})-f(E({\pi},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}})\right]$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | ------------------------------ | ------------------------------ | --- |
|  |                                | $\displaystyle=$ | $\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[f(E({{\pi}@{\pi^{\prime}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}})-f(E({\pi},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}})\ &#124;\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\right]\cdot\mathbb{P}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\right]$ |  |
|  |                                | $\displaystyle=$ | $\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[f(\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})\cup\left\{e\right\},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}})-f(\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}})\ &#124;\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\right]\cdot\mathbb{P}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\right]$ |  |
|  |                                | $\displaystyle=$ | $\displaystyle\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)\cdot\mathbb{P}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\right]$ |  |

The last term is negative, as $\mathbb{P}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\right]>0$ and $\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)<0$ by assumption.
Therefore$f_{\text{avg}}(\pi)>f_{\text{avg}}({\pi}@{\pi^{\prime}})=f_{\text{avg}}({\pi^{\prime}}@{\pi})$, which completes the proof.

### 15.2 Adaptive Data Dependent Bounds with Costs

The adaptive data dependent bound has the following generalization with costs.

###### Lemma 37 (The Adaptive Data Dependent Bound with Costs)

Suppose we have made observations ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$ after selecting $\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})$. Let $\pi^{*}$ be any policy.
Then for adaptive monotone submodular $f:2^{E}\times O^{E}\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}$

|  | $\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{\pi^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)\ \leq\ Z\ \leq\ c\left({{\pi^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)\max_{e}\left({\frac{\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)}{c(e)}}\right)$ |  | (30) |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- | ---- |

where $Z=\max_{w}\left\{\sum_{e\in E}w_{e}\,\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)\ :\ \sum_{e}c(e)w_{e}\leq c\left({{\pi^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)\text{ and }\forall e\in E,0\leq w_{e}\leq 1\right\}$.

Proof  Order the items in $\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})$ arbitrarily, and
consider the policy $\pi$ that for each $e\in\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})$ in
order selects $e$, terminating if ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}(e)\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}(e)$ and
proceeding otherwise, and, should it succeed in selecting all of$\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})$ without terminating (which occurs iff ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$), then proceeds to run $\pi^{*}$ as if from a fresh start,
forgetting the observations in ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$.
By construction
the expected marginal benefit of running the $\pi^{*}$ portion of$\pi$ conditioned on ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$ equals $\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{\pi^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)$.
For all $e\in E$, let $w(e)=\mathbb{P}\left[e\in E({\pi},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}})\ \mid\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\right]$ be the probability that $e$ is selected when running $\pi$,
conditioned on ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$.
Whenever some $e\in E\setminus\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})$ is selected by $\pi$, the current partial realization${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}$ contains ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$ as a subrealization; hence adaptive submodularity implies $\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}}\right)\leq\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)$.
It follows that the total contribution of $e$ to $\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{\pi^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)$ is upper
bounded by $w(e)\cdot\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)$.
Summing over $e\in E\setminus\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})$,
we get a bound of$\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{\pi^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)\leq\sum_{e\in E\setminus\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})}w(e)\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)$.
Next, note that each $e\in E\setminus\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})$ contributes $w(e)c(e)$ cost to $c\left({{\pi^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)$.
Hence it must be the case that$\sum_{e\in E\setminus\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})}w(e)c(e)\leq c\left({{\pi^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)$.
Obviously, $w(e)\in[0,1]$ for all $e$, since $w(e)$ is a probability.
Hence $\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{\pi^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)\leq\sum_{e\in E\setminus\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})}w(e)\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)\leq Z$ because setting $w_{e}=w(e)$ is feasible for the
the linear program for which $Z$ is the optimal value.

To show $Z\leq c\left({{\pi^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)\max_{e}\left({\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)/c(e)}\right)$, consider any feasible solution $w$ to the linear program
defining $Z$. It attains objective value

|  | $\sum_{e\in E}w_{e}\,\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)\leq\sum_{e\in E}w_{e}c(e)\frac{\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)}{c(e)}\leq\sum_{e\in E}w_{e}c(e)\max_{e\in E}\left({\frac{\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)}{c(e)}}\right)\leq c\left({{\pi^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)\max_{e\in E}\left({\frac{\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)}{c(e)}}\right)$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- |

since $\sum_{e\in E}w_{e}c(e)\leq c\left({{\pi^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)$ by
the feasibility of $w$.

A simple greedy algorithm can be used to compute $Z$; we provide
pseudocode for it in Algorithm [3](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#algorithm3 "In 15.2 Adaptive Data Dependent Bounds with Costs ‣ 15 Additional Proofs and Incorporating Item Costs ‣ Adaptive Submodularity: Theory and Applications in Active Learning and Stochastic Optimization").
The correctness of this algorithm is more readily discerned upon rewriting
the linear program using variables $x_{e}=c(e)w_{e}$ to obtain

|  | $Z=\max_{x}\left\{\sum_{e\in E}x_{e}\left({\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)/c(e)}\right)\ :\ \sum_{e}x_{e}\leq c\left({{\pi^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)\text{ and }\forall e\in E,0\leq x_{e}\leq c(e)\right\}.$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- |

Intuitively, it is clear that to optimize $x$ we should shift mass
towards variables with the highest $\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)/c(e)$ ratio.
Clearly, any optimal solution has $\sum_{e}x_{e}=c\left({{\pi^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)$.
Moreover, in any optimal solution,$\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)/c(e)>\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e^{\prime}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)/c(e^{\prime})$ implies$x_{e}=c(e)$ or $x_{e^{\prime}}=0$,
since otherwise it would be possible to shift mass from $x_{e^{\prime}}$ to$x_{e}$ and obtain an increase in objective value.
If the $\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)/c(e)$ values are distinct for distinct items,
there will be a unique solution satisfying these constraints, which
Algorithm [3](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#algorithm3 "In 15.2 Adaptive Data Dependent Bounds with Costs ‣ 15 Additional Proofs and Incorporating Item Costs ‣ Adaptive Submodularity: Theory and Applications in Active Learning and Stochastic Optimization") will compute.
Otherwise, we imagine perturbing each $\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)$ by
independent random quantities $\epsilon_{e}$ drawn uniformly from$[0,\epsilon]$ to make them distinct. This changes the optimum value
by at most $&#124;E&#124;\epsilon$, which vanishes as we let $\epsilon$ tend
towards zero. Hence any solution satisfying $\sum_{e}x_{e}=c\left({{\pi^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)$ and $\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)/c(e)>\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e^{\prime}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)/c(e^{\prime})$ implies$x_{e}=c(e)$ or $x_{e^{\prime}}=0$ is optimal.
Since Algorithm [3](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#algorithm3 "In 15.2 Adaptive Data Dependent Bounds with Costs ‣ 15 Additional Proofs and Incorporating Item Costs ‣ Adaptive Submodularity: Theory and Applications in Active Learning and Stochastic Optimization") outputs the value of such a
solution, it is correct.

Input: Groundset $E$; Partial realization ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$; Costs$c:E\to\mathbb{N}$; Budget $C=c\left({{\pi^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)$;
Conditional expected marginal benefits $\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)$ for all $e\in E$.

Output: $Z=\max_{w}\left\{\sum_{e\in E}w_{e}\,\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)\ :\ \sum_{e}c(e)w_{e}\leq c\left({{\pi^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)\text{ and }\forall e\in E,0\leq w_{e}\leq 1\right\}$

begin Sort $E$ by$\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)/c(e)$, so that $\frac{\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e_{1}\ }\!\mid\!{\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)}{c(e_{1})}\geq\frac{\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e_{2}\ }\!\mid\!{\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)}{c(e_{2})}\geq\ldots\geq\frac{\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e_{n}\ }\!\mid\!{\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)}{c(e_{n})}$;

Set $w\leftarrow\mathbf{0}$; $i\leftarrow 0$; $a\leftarrow 0$; $z\leftarrow 0$; $e\leftarrow\textsc{null}$;

while *$a<C$* do $i\leftarrow i+1$; $e\leftarrow e_{i}$;

$w_{e}\leftarrow\min\left\{1,C-a\right\}$;

$a\leftarrow a+c(e)w_{e}$; $z\leftarrow z+w_{e}\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)$;

Output $z$;end

*Algorithm 3 Algorithm to compute the
data dependent bound $Z$ of Lemma [37](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#Thmtheorem37 "Lemma 37 (The Adaptive Data Dependent Bound with Costs) ‣ 15.2 Adaptive Data Dependent Bounds with Costs ‣ 15 Additional Proofs and Incorporating Item Costs ‣ Adaptive Submodularity: Theory and Applications in Active Learning and Stochastic Optimization").*

### 15.3 The Max-Cover Objective

With item costs, the Adaptive Stochastic Maximization problem becomes one of finding some

|  | $\pi^{*}\in\mathop{\rm arg\,max}_{\pi}f_{\text{avg}}(\pi_{[{k}]})$ |  | (31) |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- | ---- |

where $k$ is a budget on the cost of selected
items, and we define $f_{\text{avg}}(\pi)$ for a randomized policy$\pi$ to be$f_{\text{avg}}(\pi):=\mathbb{E}\left[f(E({\pi},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}})\right]$ as before,
where the expectation is now over both ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}$ and the internal
randomness of $\pi$ which determines$E({\pi},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}})$ for each ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}$.
We prove the following generalization of Theorem [5](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#Thmtheorem5 "Theorem 5 ‣ 5.1 The Maximum Coverage Objective ‣ 5 Guarantees for the Greedy Policy ‣ Adaptive Submodularity: Theory and Applications in Active Learning and Stochastic Optimization").

###### Theorem 38

Fix any $\alpha\geq 1$ and item costs $c:E\to\mathbb{N}$.
If $f$ is adaptive monotone and adaptive submodular with respect to the
distribution${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{p\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}}\right)}}$, and $\pi$ is an$\alpha$-approximate greedy policy, then for all policies $\pi^{*}$ and positive
integers $\ell$ and $k$

|  | $f_{\text{avg}}(\pi_{[{\ell}]})>\left({1-e^{-\ell/\alpha k}}\right)f_{\text{avg}}(\pi^{*}_{[{k}]})\mbox{.}$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- |

Proof  The proof goes along the lines of the performance analysis of the
greedy algorithm for maximizing a submodular function subject to
a cardinality constraint of [Nemhauser et al. (1978)](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#bib.bib54). An
extension of that analysis to $\alpha$-approximate greedy algorithms,
which is analogous to ours but for the nonadaptive case, is shown by [Goundan and Schulz (2007)](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#bib.bib28).
For brevity, we will assume without loss of generality that$\pi=\pi_{[{\ell}]}$ and $\pi^{*}=\pi^{*}_{[{k}]}$.
Then for all $i$, $0\leq i<\ell$

|  | $f_{\text{avg}}(\pi^{*})\ \ \leq\ \ f_{\text{avg}}({\pi_{[{i}]}}@{\pi^{*}})\ \ \leq\ \ f_{\text{avg}}(\pi_{[{i}]})+\alpha k\left({f_{\text{avg}}(\pi_{[{i+1}]})-f_{\text{avg}}(\pi_{[{i}]})}\right).$ |  | (32) |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- | ---- |

The first inequality is due to the adaptive monotonicity of $f$ and Lemma [36](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#Thmtheorem36 "Lemma 36 (Adaptive Monotonicity Equivalence) ‣ 15.1 Incorporating Costs: Preliminaries ‣ 15 Additional Proofs and Incorporating Item Costs ‣ Adaptive Submodularity: Theory and Applications in Active Learning and Stochastic Optimization"), from which we
may infer $f_{\text{avg}}(\pi_{2})\leq f_{\text{avg}}({\pi_{1}}@{\pi_{2}})$ for any $\pi_{1}$ and $\pi_{2}$.
The second inequality may be obtained as
a corollary of Lemma [37](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#Thmtheorem37 "Lemma 37 (The Adaptive Data Dependent Bound with Costs) ‣ 15.2 Adaptive Data Dependent Bounds with Costs ‣ 15 Additional Proofs and Incorporating Item Costs ‣ Adaptive Submodularity: Theory and Applications in Active Learning and Stochastic Optimization") as follows.
Fix any partial realization ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$ of the form$\left\{(e,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}(e)):e\in E({\pi_{[{i}]}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}})\right\}$ for some ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}$.
Consider $\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{\pi^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)$, which equals
the expected marginal benefit of the $\pi^{*}$ portion of${\pi_{[{i}]}}@{\pi^{*}}$ conditioned on ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$.
Lemma [37](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#Thmtheorem37 "Lemma 37 (The Adaptive Data Dependent Bound with Costs) ‣ 15.2 Adaptive Data Dependent Bounds with Costs ‣ 15 Additional Proofs and Incorporating Item Costs ‣ Adaptive Submodularity: Theory and Applications in Active Learning and Stochastic Optimization") allows us to bound it as

|  | $\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{\pi^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)\right]\ \leq\ \mathbb{E}\left[c\left({{\pi^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)\right]\cdot\max_{e}\left({\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)/c(e)}\right),$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- |

where the expectations are taken
over the internal randomness of $\pi^{*}$, if there is any. Note
that since $\pi^{*}$ has the form $\pi^{\prime}_{[{k}]}$ for some$\pi^{\prime}$ we know that for all ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}$,$\mathbb{E}\left[c(E({\pi^{*}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}}))\right]\leq k$, where the
expectation is again taken over the internal randomness of $\pi^{*}$.
Hence $\mathbb{E}\left[c\left({{\pi^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)\right]\leq k$ for all ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$. It
follows that $\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{\pi^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)\right]\leq k\cdot\max_{e}\left({\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)/c(e)}\right)$.
By definition of an $\alpha$-approximate greedy policy, $\pi$ obtains at least $(1/\alpha)\max_{e}\left({\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)/c(e)}\right)\geq\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{\pi^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)\right]/\alpha k$ expected marginal benefit per unit cost
in a step immediately following its observation of ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$.
Next we take an appropriate convex combination of the previous inequality
with different values of ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$.
Let ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Psi}}$ be a random partial realization distributed as${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{p_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Psi}}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}\right)}}:=\mathbb{P}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Psi}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\mid\exists{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}.\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}=\left\{(e,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}(e)):e\in E({\pi_{[{i}]}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}})\right\}\right]$ Then

|  | $\displaystyle f_{\text{avg}}(\pi_{[{i+1}]})-f_{\text{avg}}(\pi_{[{i}]})$ | $\displaystyle\geq$ | $\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\alpha}\,\max_{e}\left({\frac{\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Psi}}}}\right)}{c(e)}}\right)\right]$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | ------------------------------ | ------------------------------ | --- |
|  |                                | $\displaystyle\geq$ | $\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{\pi^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Psi}}}}\right)\right]}{\alpha k}\right]$ |  |
|  |                                | $\displaystyle=$ | $\displaystyle\frac{f_{\text{avg}}({\pi_{[{i}]}}@{\pi^{*}})-f_{\text{avg}}(\pi_{[{i}]})}{\alpha k}$ |  |

A simple rearrangement of terms then yields the second inequality in ([32](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#S15.E32 "In 15.3 The Max-Cover Objective ‣ 15 Additional Proofs and Incorporating Item Costs ‣ Adaptive Submodularity: Theory and Applications in Active Learning and Stochastic Optimization")).

Now define $\Delta_{i}:=f_{\text{avg}}(\pi^{*})-f_{\text{avg}}(\pi_{[{i}]})$,
so that ([32](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#S15.E32 "In 15.3 The Max-Cover Objective ‣ 15 Additional Proofs and Incorporating Item Costs ‣ Adaptive Submodularity: Theory and Applications in Active Learning and Stochastic Optimization")) implies $\Delta_{i}\leq\alpha k(\Delta_{i}-\Delta_{i+1})$, from which we infer$\Delta_{i+1}\leq\left({1-\frac{1}{\alpha k}}\right)\Delta_{i}$ and hence$\Delta_{\ell}\leq\left({1-\frac{1}{\alpha k}}\right)^{\ell}\Delta_{0}<e^{-\ell/\alpha k}\Delta_{0}$, where for this last inequality we have used
the fact that $1-x<e^{-x}$ for all$x>0$.
Thus$f_{\text{avg}}(\pi^{*})-f_{\text{avg}}(\pi_{[{\ell}]})<e^{-\ell/\alpha k}\left({f_{\text{avg}}(\pi^{*})-f_{\text{avg}}(\pi_{[{0}]})}\right)\leq e^{-\ell/\alpha k}f_{\text{avg}}(\pi^{*})$ so$f_{\text{avg}}(\pi)>(1-e^{-\ell/\alpha k})f_{\text{avg}}(\pi^{*})$.



### 15.4 The Min-Cost-Cover Objective

In this section, we provide arbitrary item cost generalizations
of Theorem [13](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#Thmtheorem13 "Theorem 13 ‣ 5.2.1 Minimizing the Average Cost ‣ 5.2 The Min Cost Cover Objective ‣ 5 Guarantees for the Greedy Policy ‣ Adaptive Submodularity: Theory and Applications in Active Learning and Stochastic Optimization") and
Theorem [14](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#Thmtheorem14 "Theorem 14 ‣ 5.2.2 Minimizing the Worst-Case Cost ‣ 5.2 The Min Cost Cover Objective ‣ 5 Guarantees for the Greedy Policy ‣ Adaptive Submodularity: Theory and Applications in Active Learning and Stochastic Optimization").
With item costs the Adaptive Stochastic Minimum Cost Cover problem
becomes one of finding, for some quota on utility $Q$,

|  | $\pi^{*}\in\mathop{\rm arg\,min}_{\pi}{c_{\text{avg}}{(\pi)}}\text{ such that }f(E({\pi},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}})\geq Q\text{ for all }{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}},$ |  | (33) |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- | ---- |

where ${c_{\text{avg}}{(\pi)}}:=\mathbb{E}\left[c(E({\pi},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}}))\right]$.
Without loss of generality, we may take a truncated version of $f$,
namely $(A,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}})\mapsto\min\left\{Q,f(A,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}})\right\}$, and
rephrase Problem ([33](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#S15.E33 "In 15.4 The Min-Cost-Cover Objective ‣ 15 Additional Proofs and Incorporating Item Costs ‣ Adaptive Submodularity: Theory and Applications in Active Learning and Stochastic Optimization")) as finding

|  | $\pi^{*}\in\mathop{\rm arg\,min}_{\pi}{c_{\text{avg}}{(\pi)}}\text{ such that }\pi\text{ covers }{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}\text{ for all }{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}.$ |  | (34) |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- | ---- |

Hereby, recall that $\pi$ covers ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}$ if$\mathbb{E}\left[f(E({\pi},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}})\right]=f(E,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}})$,
where the expectation is over any internal randomness of $\pi$.
We will consider only
Problem ([34](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#S15.E34 "In 15.4 The Min-Cost-Cover Objective ‣ 15 Additional Proofs and Incorporating Item Costs ‣ Adaptive Submodularity: Theory and Applications in Active Learning and Stochastic Optimization")) for the
remainder.
We also consider the worst-case variant of this problem, where we
replace the expected cost ${c_{\text{avg}}{(\pi)}}$ objective with the worst-case cost${c_{\text{wc}}{(\pi)}}:=\max_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}}{c(E({\pi},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}}))}$.

The definition of coverage (Definition [7](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#Thmtheorem7 "Definition 7 (Coverage) ‣ 5.2 The Min Cost Cover Objective ‣ 5 Guarantees for the Greedy Policy ‣ Adaptive Submodularity: Theory and Applications in Active Learning and Stochastic Optimization") in §[5.2](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#S5.SS2 "5.2 The Min Cost Cover Objective ‣ 5 Guarantees for the Greedy Policy ‣ Adaptive Submodularity: Theory and Applications in Active Learning and Stochastic Optimization") on page [7](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#Thmtheorem7 "Definition 7 (Coverage) ‣ 5.2 The Min Cost Cover Objective ‣ 5 Guarantees for the Greedy Policy ‣ Adaptive Submodularity: Theory and Applications in Active Learning and Stochastic Optimization")) requires no
modification to handle item costs.
Note, however, that coverage is all-or-nothing in the sense that
covering a realization ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}$ with probability less than one does not count
as covering it. A corollary of this is that
only items whose runs have finished help with coverage,
whereas currently running items do not. For a simple example,
consider the case where $E=\left\{e\right\}$, $c(e)=2$,$f(A,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}})=&#124;A&#124;$, and
policy $\pi$ that selects $e$ and then
terminates. Then $\pi_{[{1}]}$ is a randomized policy which is$\pi$ with probability $\frac{1}{2}$, and is the empty policy with
probability $\frac{1}{2}$, so$\mathbb{E}\left[f(E({\pi},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}})\right]=\frac{1}{2}<1=f(E,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}})$ for each ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}$. Hence, even though half the time$\pi_{[{1}]}$ covers all realizations, it is counted as not
covering any.

We begin with a claim relating pointwise submodularity to strong adaptive
submodularity.

###### Lemma 39

If $f$ is adaptive submodular with respect to ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{p\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}}\right)}}$ and $f$ is pointwise
submodular meaning $S\mapsto f(S,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}})$ is submodular for all ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}$,
then $f$ is strongly adaptive submodular with respect to ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{p\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}}\right)}}$.

Proof  By assumption $f$ is adaptive submodular with respect to ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{p\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}}\right)}}$, so it is
sufficient to prove that Eq. ([16](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#S5.E16 "In Definition 12 (Strong Adaptive Submodularity) ‣ 5.2.1 Minimizing the Average Cost ‣ 5.2 The Min Cost Cover Objective ‣ 5 Guarantees for the Greedy Policy ‣ Adaptive Submodularity: Theory and Applications in Active Learning and Stochastic Optimization")) holds, i.e.,$\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}}\right)\geq\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}}\right)$.
Fix any ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}$ and $e\in E$.
Let $\delta_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}}(e,S):=f(S\cup\left\{e\right\},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}})-f(S,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}})$.
From the definition of $\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}}\right)$ and $\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}}\right)$,
we have

|  | $\displaystyle\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}}\right)$ | $\displaystyle=$ | $\displaystyle\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}}\mathbb{P}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}\right]\delta_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}}(e,\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}))$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | ------------------------------ | ------------------------------ | --- |
|  |                                | $\displaystyle\geq$ | $\displaystyle\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}}\mathbb{P}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}\right]\delta_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}}(e,\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}))$ |  |
|  |                                | $\displaystyle=$ | $\displaystyle\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}}\right)$ |  |

where we have used$\delta_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}}(e,\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}))\geq\delta_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}}(e,\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}))$ by
the pointwise submodularity of $f$.

Now we provide an approximation guarantee for the average-case
policy cost with arbitrary item costs.

###### Theorem 40

Suppose $f:2^{E}\times O^{E}\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}$ is strongly adaptive submodular and strongly adaptive
monotone with respect to ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{p\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}}\right)}}$ and there exists $Q$ such that$f(E,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}})=Q$ for all ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}$.
Let $\eta$ be any value such that$f(S,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}})>Q-\eta$ implies $f(S,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}})=Q$ for all $S$ and${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}$.
Let $\delta=\min_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{p\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}}\right)}}$ be the minimum probability of
any realization.
Let $\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}$ be an optimal policy
minimizing the expected cost of items selected
to guarantee every realization is covered.
Let $\pi$ be an $\alpha$-approximate
greedy policy with respect to the item costs.
Then in general

|  | ${c_{\text{avg}}{(\pi)}}\leq\alpha\,{c_{\text{avg}}{(\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*})}}\left({\ln\left({\frac{Q}{\delta\eta}}\right)+1}\right)^{2}$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- |

and for self–certifying instances

|  | ${c_{\text{avg}}{(\pi)}}\leq\alpha\,{c_{\text{avg}}{(\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*})}}\left({\ln\left({\frac{Q}{\eta}}\right)+1}\right)^{2}\mbox{.}$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- |

Note that if $\operatorname{range}(f)\subset\mathbb{Z}$, then $\eta=1$ is a valid
choice, so for general and
self–certifying instances we have ${c_{\text{avg}}{(\pi)}}\leq\alpha\,{c_{\text{avg}}{(\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*})}}\left({\ln(Q/\delta)+1}\right)^{2}$ and ${c_{\text{avg}}{(\pi)}}\leq\alpha\,{c_{\text{avg}}{(\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*})}}\left({\ln(Q)+1}\right)^{2}$, respectively.

### Proof of Theorem [40](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#Thmtheorem40 "Theorem 40 ‣ 15.4 The Min-Cost-Cover Objective ‣ 15 Additional Proofs and Incorporating Item Costs ‣ Adaptive Submodularity: Theory and Applications in Active Learning and Stochastic Optimization")

We first require some additional definitions.
We extend $f$ by defining$f(A,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})$ via

|  | $f(A,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}):=\mathbb{E}\left[f(A,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}})\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\right]$ |  | (35) |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- | ---- |

Further, let

|  | $f({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}):=f(\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})=\mathbb{E}\left[f(\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}})\ \mid\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\right]$ |  | (36) |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- | ---- |

###### Definition 41 (Execution Trace)

Given a policy $\pi$ and a realization ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}$, let the *trace* $\tau=\tau({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}})$ of$\pi$ under ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}$ be the sequence of partial realizations specifying
its set of observations through time, i.e.,${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{0}\subset{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{1}\subset\cdots\subset{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{\ell}$, such that $\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{i})\setminus\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{i-1})$ consists precisely of the $i^{\mathrm{th}}$ item selected by$\pi$.

###### Definition 42 (Execution Arborescence)

For a policy $\pi$, define its *execution arborescence* as the
digraph$(\dom(\pi),A)$ with vertices $\dom(\pi)$ and edges

|  | $A:=\left\{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}):\exists{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\cup\left\{(\pi({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}(\pi({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})))\right\}\right\}.$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- |

In other words, its vertices are the partial realizations that may be encountered by $\pi$, and it has a directed edge from ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$ to ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}$ if it may immediately encounter ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}$ after ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$ in some execution trace.

###### Definition 43 (Spanning Edges, Sources, Targets, and Successors)

Given a policy $\pi$ with execution arborescence with arcs $A$, and some positive $x\in\mathbb{R}$, let $A_{x}$ be
the edges that span $x$ expected benefit, i.e.,

|  | $A_{x}:=\left\{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}):({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime})\in A,\ f({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})<x\leq f({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime})\right\}$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- |

Further let $U_{x}$ be the *sources* of edges in $A_{x}$ and $V_{x}$ be the *targets*.
That is, $U_{x}:=\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}:\exists{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime},({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime})\in A_{x}\right\}$ and$V_{x}:=\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}:\exists{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime})\in A_{x}\right\}$.
Finally, the *successors* $S_{x}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})$ of some ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\in U_{x}$ are
the sources ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}$ that are minimal supersets of ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$ in $U_{x}$ when
interpreting partial realizations as set of (item, observation) pairs. Formally,

|  | $S_{x}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}):=\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}\in U_{x},\nexists{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\subset{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime\prime}\subset{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}\right\}$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- |

###### Definition 44 (Concatenative Pseudopolicies)

Given two policies $\pi$ and $\pi^{*}$ and some nonnegative $x\in\mathbb{R}$, we define the *concatenative pseudopolicy* ${{\pi}[x]}@{\pi^{*}}$ as the stochastic process that, on each
realization ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}$, runs policy $\pi$ until it is just about to achieve $x$ expected reward
(formally, until reaching ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\left({{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}},{x}}\right)$),
and then runs $\pi^{*}$ as if from scratch.
Hence on realization ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}$ it will play$\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\left({{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}},{x}}\right))\cup E({\pi^{*}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}})$ where ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\left({{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}},{x}}\right)$ is defined relative to$\pi$.

Note that ${{\pi}[x]}@{\pi^{*}}$ isn’t a proper policy, because it decides when to switch to executing$\pi^{*}$ based on information about ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}$ that it cannot infer from its current partial realization.
Specifically, upon reaching ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\in U_{x}$ it will select $\pi^{*}(\emptyset)$ if there exists${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}$ such that $({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime})\in A_{x}$ and ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}$, and will select$\pi({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})$ otherwise.

#### The Strategy

Our overall strategy will be to bound the expected cost ${c_{\text{avg}}{(\pi)}}$ of$\pi$ by bounding the price $\theta$ it pays per unit of *expected reward* gained as it runs (measured as $\mathbb{E}\left[f(\dom({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}})\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}\sim{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}\right]$ where ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$ is the current
partial realization) and then integrating over the run. To bound the price$\pi$ pays, we define an alternative cost scheme for policies, bound the
price $\pi$ pays in terms of the alternative cost of an optimal policy$\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}$, and finally bound the alternative cost of $\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}$ in terms of its
actual cost.

#### Progress bounds

First, observe that

|  | $\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}}\right)\geq Q-x\text{ for all }({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime})\in A_{x}$ |  | (37) |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- | ---- |

This holds because$f({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})<x$ by definition of $A_{x}$, and$f(E({\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}})=Q$ for all ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}$ since $\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}$ covers
every realization. Since $Q$ is the maximum possible reward,
if $\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}}\right)<Q-x$ then we can generate a
violation of strong adaptive monotonicity by
fixing some ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}^{\prime}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}$, selecting$E({\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}^{\prime}})$, and then selecting$\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})$ to reduce the expected reward.

The bound in Eq. ([37](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#S15.E37 "In Progress bounds ‣ Proof of Theorem ‣ 15 Additional Proofs and Incorporating Item Costs ‣ Adaptive Submodularity: Theory and Applications in Active Learning and Stochastic Optimization")) is useful when $x$ is far from$Q$, however we will require a stronger bound for $x$ very close to $Q$.
We will use slightly different analyses for general instances and for
self–certifying instances.

We begin with general instances.
For these, we will prove a stronger bound for obtaining the final$\delta\eta$ reward, i.e., for $x\in[Q-\delta\eta,Q]$.

|  | $\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}}\right)\geq\delta\eta\text{ for all }({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime})\in A_{x}$ |  | (38) |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- | ---- |

Fix ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\in\dom(\pi)$ and any ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}^{\prime}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$.
We say ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$ *covers* ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}^{\prime}$ if$\pi$ covers ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}^{\prime}$ by the time it observes ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$.
By definition of $\delta$ and $\eta$, if some ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}^{\prime}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}$ is
not covered by ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$ then$Q-f(\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime})\geq\delta\eta$.
Hence the last item that $\pi$ selects, say upon observing${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$, must increase its conditional expected
value from $f(\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime})\leq Q-\delta\eta$ to $Q$ for all ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}$.

For self–certifying instances we show that

|  | $\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}}\right)\geq\eta\text{ for all }({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime})\in A_{x}$ |  | (39) |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- | ---- |

using a similar argument for $x\in[Q-\eta,Q]$.
We first argue that
the last item that $\pi$ selects must increase its conditional expected
value from at most $Q-\eta$ to $Q$.
For suppose $\pi$ currently observes ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$, and has not achieved
conditional value $Q$, i.e., $f({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})<Q$.
Then some ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$ is uncovered.
Since the instance is self–certifying, *every* ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}$ with${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$ is uncovered, and has$f(\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}})<f(E,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}})=Q$. By definition of $\eta$, for each ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}$ with${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$ we then have $f(\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}})\leq Q-\eta$, which
implies $f(\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime})\leq Q-\eta$.

#### Upper bounding the price $\pi$ pays with Alternate Costs

Next we upper-bound the price $\pi$ pays in terms of an alternative pricing
scheme, $\hat{c}$, for $\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}$. Note the cost of $\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}$ under $\hat{c}$ may be higher than ${c_{\text{avg}}{(\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*})}}$.
We will eventually bound the alternative cost in terms of the true cost.

Fix $x$ and any $e$ that may be played by $\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}$ under some realization ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}$.
Consider any $({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime})\in A_{x}$ with ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}$.
Fix some ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}\in\dom(\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*})$ such that $e=\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e})$.
We charge $e$ a price of $c(e)/\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}}}\right)$ per-unit reward in this case.
Hence, under realization ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}$,
the alternative cost is

|  | $c(e)\left({\frac{f\left({\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})\cup\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e})\cup\left\{e\right\},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}}\right)-f\left({\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})\cup\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}}\right)}{\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}}}\right)}}\right)$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- |

Taking the expectation over ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}$ such that ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}$ and ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}$ (which is equivalent to ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}$) yields an
expected alternative cost of

|  | $\hat{c}_{e}\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}\right):=c(e)\left({\frac{\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}}}\right)}{\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}}}\right)}}\right)$ |  | (40) |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- | ---- |

For brevity, we will define

|  | $\Delta_{a,e}:=\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}}}\right)$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- |

for arc$a=({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime})\in A_{x}$ and ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}$.
Note we could have multiple ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}$ such that $\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e})=e$,
however for notational convenience we can imagine creating separate copies of$e$ for each such partial realization and assume without loss of generality that
for each item there is a unique partial realization that $\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}$ plays it
under; That is, for each $e$ there is at most one ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}$ such that $\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e})=e$.

Next, for arc $a=({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime})$ define $w_{a,e}$ to be the probability
that $\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}$ plays $e$ conditioned on ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}$:

|  | $w_{a,e}:=\mathbb{P}\left[e\in E({\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}})\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}\right]=\mathbb{P}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}\right]$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- |

Next, consider the cost $\hat{c}\left({{\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}}\right)$ which is the expected
alternative cost paid by $\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}$ starting from $\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})$ and conditioned on ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}$ for some fixed$x$ and $a=({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime})\in A_{x}$.
Note that since the alternative cost depends on $x$ and $a$, this alternative
cost does as well.
Formally,

|  | $\hat{c}\left({{\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}}\right):=\sum_{e}w_{a,e}\hat{c}_{e}\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}\right)$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- |

The expected benefit of running $\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}$ starting from ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$ and conditioning on ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}$ is$\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}}\right):=\sum_{e}w_{a,e}\Delta_{a,e}$.
Hence

|  | $\displaystyle\hat{c}\left({{\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}}\right)$ | $\displaystyle=$ | $\displaystyle\sum_{e}w_{a,e}\hat{c}_{e}\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}\right)$ |  | (41) |
| --- | ------------------------------ | ------------------------------ | ------------------------------ | --- | ---- |
|  |                                | $\displaystyle=$ | $\displaystyle\sum_{e}w_{a,e}c(e)\left({\frac{\Delta_{a,e}}{\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}}}\right)}}\right)$ |  | (42) |
|  |                                | $\displaystyle\geq$ | $\displaystyle\sum_{e}w_{a,e}\Delta_{a,e}\min_{e^{\prime}}\left({\frac{c(e^{\prime})}{\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e^{\prime}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}}}\right)}}\right)$ |  | (43) |
|  |                                | $\displaystyle\geq$ | $\displaystyle\sum_{e}w_{a,e}\Delta_{a,e}\min_{e^{\prime}}\left({\frac{c(e^{\prime})}{\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e^{\prime}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)}}\right)$ |  | (44) |
|  |                                | $\displaystyle=$ | $\displaystyle\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}}\right)\min_{e^{\prime}}\left({\frac{c(e^{\prime})}{\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e^{\prime}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)}}\right)$ |  | (45) |

Hence

|  | $\min_{e^{\prime}}\left({\frac{c(e^{\prime})}{\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e^{\prime}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)}}\right)\leq\frac{\hat{c}\left({{\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}}\right)}{\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}}\right)}$ |  | (46) |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- | ---- |

Next, note that$V_{x}:=\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}:\exists{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime})\in A_{x}\right\}$ partitions the set of realizations.
We define the alternative cost of $\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}$ as

|  | ${\hat{c}_{\text{avg}}{(\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*})}}:=\sum_{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime})\in A_{x}}\hat{c}\left({{\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}}\right)\mathbb{P}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}\right]$ |  | (47) |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- | ---- |

Note it too is implicitly dependent on $x$.

#### Bounding the Alternate Costs

Next we bound ${\hat{c}_{\text{avg}}{(\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*})}}$ in terms of ${c_{\text{avg}}{(\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*})}}$ and problem
parameters $Q,\eta$ and $\delta$.
Ultimately we will show that for general instances

|  | ${\hat{c}_{\text{avg}}{(\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*})}}\leq{c_{\text{avg}}{(\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*})}}\left({\ln\left({\frac{Q}{\delta\eta}}\right)+1}\right)$ |  | (48) |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- | ---- |

and for self–certifying instances

|  | ${\hat{c}_{\text{avg}}{(\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*})}}\leq{c_{\text{avg}}{(\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*})}}\left({\ln\left({\frac{Q}{\eta}}\right)+1}\right).$ |  | (49) |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- | ---- |

It is sufficient to prove this bound element by element.
That is, we define

|  | $\hat{c}(e):=\sum_{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime})\in A_{x}}\hat{c}_{e}\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}\right)\mathbb{P}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}\right]$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- |

and prove $\hat{c}(e)/c(e)\leq\left({\ln\left({\frac{Q}{\delta\eta}}\right)+1}\right)$ for general instances, and $\hat{c}(e)/c(e)\leq\left({\ln\left({\frac{Q}{\eta}}\right)+1}\right)$ for self–certifying instances.

To simplify the exposition we define some additional notation.
Fix $\pi$, $\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}$, $x$, and $e$.
Let $q[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]:=\mathbb{P}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}\right]$ and let

|  | $q^{\prime}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]:=\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}:({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime})\in A_{x}}q[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}]$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- |

Let $\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]:=\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}}}\right)$.
Let

|  | $\delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]:=\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}:({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime})\in A_{x}}\left({\frac{q[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}]}{q^{\prime}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]}}\right)\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}}}\right)$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- |

be the conditional expected benefit of selecting $e$ in the concatenative pseudopolicy ${{\pi}[x]}@{\pi^{*}}$ immediately upon seeing ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$ (see definition [44](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#Thmtheorem44 "Definition 44 (Concatenative Pseudopolicies) ‣ Proof of Theorem ‣ 15 Additional Proofs and Incorporating Item Costs ‣ Adaptive Submodularity: Theory and Applications in Active Learning and Stochastic Optimization")).

By construction, $\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}\in V_{x}\right\}$ partitions the set of all
realizations (since for each ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}$ there is a unique edge in the execution
arborescence where the policy passes $x$ expected reward in the trace$\tau\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}}\right)$ as the policy runs). Furthermore, conditioned on$\exists({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime})\in A_{x}$ such that ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}$,
we pay$c(e)\delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]/\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]$. Taking the expectation over${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\in U_{x}$, we obtain

|  | $\frac{\hat{c}(e)}{c(e)}=\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\in U_{x}}\frac{q^{\prime}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]\delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]}{\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]}$ |  | (50) |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- | ---- |

Next we need a subclaim about how $\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]$ relates to to$\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}]$ for ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\subset{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}$. Recall the definition of$S_{x}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})$ (from definition [43](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#Thmtheorem43 "Definition 43 (Spanning Edges, Sources, Targets, and Successors) ‣ Proof of Theorem ‣ 15 Additional Proofs and Incorporating Item Costs ‣ Adaptive Submodularity: Theory and Applications in Active Learning and Stochastic Optimization")).

###### Lemma 45

Fix $\pi$, $\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}$, $x$, and $e$ and let $p_{i}$, $p^{\prime}_{i}$, $\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]$, and$\delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]$ be as above. For any ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\in U_{x}$,
if for some $\epsilon\in\mathbb{R}$

|  | $q^{\prime}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]\delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]\geq\left({q^{\prime}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]+\epsilon\left({q[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]-q^{\prime}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]}\right)}\right)\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- |

then

|  | $\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}]\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}\in S_{x}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})\right]:=\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}\in S_{x}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})}\mathbb{P}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}\right]\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}]\ \leq\ \Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]\left({1-\epsilon}\right)$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- |

Proof  Fix ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$.
We claim that by strong adaptive submodularity,

|  | $q[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]\ \geq\ q^{\prime}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]\delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]+\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{v}}\in S_{x}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})}q[{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{v}}}]\Delta[{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{v}}}]$ |  | (51) |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- | ---- |

To see this, note that $\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}:({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime})\in A_{x}\right\}\cup\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{v}}\in S_{x}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})\right\}$ partitions the set of realizations, and thus

|  | $\displaystyle q[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]$ | $\displaystyle=$ | $\displaystyle q^{\prime}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]\delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]+\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{v}}\in S_{x}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})}\mathbb{P}\left[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{v}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}\right]\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e};{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{v}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}}}\right)$ |  | (52) |
| --- | ------------------------------ | ------------------------------ | ------------------------------ | --- | ---- |
|  |                                | $\displaystyle\geq$ | $\displaystyle q^{\prime}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]\delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]+\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{v}}\in S_{x}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})}\mathbb{P}\left[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{v}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}\right]\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{v}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}}}\right)$ |  | (53) |
|  |                                | $\displaystyle\equiv$ | $\displaystyle q^{\prime}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]\delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]+\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{v}}\in S_{x}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})}q[{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{v}}}]\Delta[{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{v}}}]$ |  | (54) |

Given Eq. ([51](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#S15.E51 "In Bounding the Alternate Costs ‣ Proof of Theorem ‣ 15 Additional Proofs and Incorporating Item Costs ‣ Adaptive Submodularity: Theory and Applications in Active Learning and Stochastic Optimization")) and the assumption that$q^{\prime}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]\delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]\geq\left({q^{\prime}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]+(q[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]-q^{\prime}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}])\epsilon}\right)\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]$,
we have

|  | $q[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]\ \geq\ \left({q^{\prime}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]+(q[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]-q^{\prime}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}])\epsilon}\right)\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]+\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{v}}\in S_{x}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})}q[{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{v}}}]\Delta[{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{v}}}]$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- |

Hence

|  | $\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{v}}\in S_{x}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})}q[{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{v}}}]\Delta[{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{v}}}]\leq(q[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]-q^{\prime}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}])(1-\epsilon)\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- |

Dividing by $(q[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]-q^{\prime}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}])$ yields

|  | $\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{v}}\in S_{x}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})}\mathbb{P}\left[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{v}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}\right]\Delta[{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{v}}}]=\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{v}}\in S_{x}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})}\left({\frac{q[{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{v}}}]}{q[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]-q^{\prime}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]}}\right)\Delta[{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{v}}}]\leq(1-\epsilon)\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- |

which completes the proof.

Recall we wish to upper-bound $\frac{\hat{c}(e)}{c(e)}$ using
Eq. ([50](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#S15.E50 "In Bounding the Alternate Costs ‣ Proof of Theorem ‣ 15 Additional Proofs and Incorporating Item Costs ‣ Adaptive Submodularity: Theory and Applications in Active Learning and Stochastic Optimization")).
Let $\varepsilon({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}})\in\mathbb{R}$ be set such that

|  | $q^{\prime}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]\delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]=\left({q^{\prime}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]+\varepsilon({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}})\left({q[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]-q^{\prime}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]}\right)}\right)\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- |

for all ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$.
Then

|  | $\displaystyle\frac{\hat{c}(e)}{c(e)}$ | $\displaystyle=$ | $\displaystyle\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\in U_{x}}\frac{q^{\prime}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]\delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]}{\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]}$ |  | (55) |
| --- | ------------------------------ | ------------------------------ | ------------------------------ | --- | ---- |
|  |                                | $\displaystyle=$ | $\displaystyle\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\in U_{x}}\left({q^{\prime}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]+\varepsilon({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}})\left({q[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]-q^{\prime}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]}\right)}\right)$ |  | (56) |
|  |                                | $\displaystyle=$ | $\displaystyle\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\in U_{x}}q^{\prime}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]+\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\in U_{x}}\varepsilon({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}})\left({q[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]-q^{\prime}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]}\right)$ |  | (57) |
|  |                                | $\displaystyle=$ | $\displaystyle 1+\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\in U_{x}}\varepsilon({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}})\left({q[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]-q^{\prime}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]}\right)$ |  | (58) |
|  |                                | $\displaystyle\leq$ | $\displaystyle 1+\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\in U_{x}}\varepsilon({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}})^{+}\left({q[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]-q^{\prime}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]}\right)$ |  | (59) |

where $\varepsilon({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}})^{+}:=\max\left({0,\varepsilon({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}})}\right)$.

###### Lemma 46

For general instances

|  | $\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\in U_{x}}\varepsilon({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}})^{+}\left({q[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]-q^{\prime}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]}\right)\ \leq\ \ln\left({\frac{Q}{\delta\eta}}\right)$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- |

and for self–certifying instances

|  | $\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\in U_{x}}\varepsilon({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}})^{+}\left({q[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]-q^{\prime}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]}\right)\ \leq\ \ln\left({\frac{Q}{\eta}}\right)$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- |

Proof  From Lemma [45](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#Thmtheorem45 "Lemma 45 ‣ Bounding the Alternate Costs ‣ Proof of Theorem ‣ 15 Additional Proofs and Incorporating Item Costs ‣ Adaptive Submodularity: Theory and Applications in Active Learning and Stochastic Optimization"), we have

|  | $\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}]\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}\in S_{x}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})\text{ and }{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}\right]\ \leq\ \Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]\left({1-\varepsilon({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}})}\right)$ |  | (61) |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- | ---- |

From adaptive submodularity, we also have $\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}]\leq\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]$ for all ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}\in S_{x}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})$, and hence$\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}]\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}\in S_{x}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})\text{ and }{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}\right]\ \leq\ \Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]$.
Hence

|  | $\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}]\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}\in S_{x}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})\text{ and }{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}\right]\ \leq\ \Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]\left({1-\varepsilon({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}})^{+}}\right)$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- |

Note

|  | $\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\in U_{x}}\varepsilon({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}})^{+}\left({q[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]-q^{\prime}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]}\right)\leq\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\in U_{x}}\varepsilon({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}})^{+}q[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- |

We will bound the latter sum by constructing an unbiased estimate of it, and
taking the expectation of that estimate.
Our unbiased estimator is based on a random trace $\tau({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}})$ of $\pi$

|  | $R({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}):=\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\in U_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\in\tau({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}})}\varepsilon({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}})^{+}$ |  | (62) |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- | ---- |

where ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}$ is sampled conditioned on ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}$, i.e.,
each ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}$ is sampled with probability $\mathbb{P}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}\right]$.

For any ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\in U_{x}$, the probability of seeing ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$ conditioned on$e$ being selected (i.e. ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}$) is exactly $q[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]$.
Hence

|  | $\mathbb{E}\left[R({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}})\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}\right]:=\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\in U_{x}\cap\tau({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}})}q[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]\varepsilon({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}})^{+}$ |  | (63) |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- | ---- |

Next consider the random sequence of partial realizations in$U_{x}\cap\tau({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}})$, which we will denote as${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{2},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{k}$.
We assume without loss of generality that no policy selects an item that
will give it zero reward in expectation.
Let $Y_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}})$ be a random variable such that

|  | $\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{i+1}}]=(1-Y_{i})\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{i}}]$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- |

By adaptive submodularity, $Y_{i}\geq 0$ for all $i$, and also$\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{i}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}\right]=\varepsilon({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{i}})$ for all $i$ from Eq. ([61](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#S15.E61 "In Bounding the Alternate Costs ‣ Proof of Theorem ‣ 15 Additional Proofs and Incorporating Item Costs ‣ Adaptive Submodularity: Theory and Applications in Active Learning and Stochastic Optimization")).
Next, note that because the maximum possible reward is $Q$ and the
minimum increment is $\eta$, and the minimum probability of any realization is$\delta$, we have for all ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$ $\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]\leq Q$, and$\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]>0\Rightarrow\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]\geq\delta\eta$.
Hence

|  | $\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{k}}]=\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{1}}]\prod_{i=1}^{k-1}(1-Y_{i})$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- |

implies

|  | $\prod_{i=1}^{k-1}(1-Y_{i})=\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{k}}]/\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{1}}]\geq\delta\eta/Q$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- |

Using $1-x\leq e^{x}$, we conclude

|  | $\delta\eta/Q\leq\prod_{i=1}^{k-1}(1-Y_{i})\leq\exp\left({-\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}Y_{i}}\right)$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- |

and hence $\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}Y_{i}\leq\ln\left({\frac{Q}{\delta\eta}}\right)$.
Hence in general,

|  | $\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[R({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}})\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}\right]$ | $\displaystyle=$ | $\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\in U_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\in\tau({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}})}\varepsilon({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}})^{+}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}\right]$ |  | (64) |
| --- | ------------------------------ | ------------------------------ | ------------------------------ | --- | ---- |
|  |                                | $\displaystyle=$ | $\displaystyle\sum_{i\geq 1}\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}})\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}\right]$ |  | (65) |
|  |                                | $\displaystyle=$ | $\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i\geq 1}Y_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}})\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}\right]$ |  | (66) |
|  |                                | $\displaystyle\leq$ | $\displaystyle\ln\left({\frac{Q}{\delta\eta}}\right)$ |  | (67) |

For self–certifying instances, the argument is similar, except that we have$\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]>0\Rightarrow\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}]\geq\eta$ because the the minimum
increment is $\eta$, and the reward is determined by the current partial
realization. Hence $\Delta[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{k}}]\geq\eta$, and so$\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}Y_{i}\leq\ln\left({\frac{Q}{\eta}}\right)$ in this case, so$\mathbb{E}\left[R({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}})\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}_{e}\right]\leq\ln\left({\frac{Q}{\eta}}\right)$.

#### Putting it all together

We show how to obtain the result for general instances. The result for
self–certifying instances
is strictly analogous.

From Eq. ([37](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#S15.E37 "In Progress bounds ‣ Proof of Theorem ‣ 15 Additional Proofs and Incorporating Item Costs ‣ Adaptive Submodularity: Theory and Applications in Active Learning and Stochastic Optimization")) and Eq. ([38](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#S15.E38 "In Progress bounds ‣ Proof of Theorem ‣ 15 Additional Proofs and Incorporating Item Costs ‣ Adaptive Submodularity: Theory and Applications in Active Learning and Stochastic Optimization")),
we have that for all $({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime})\in A_{x}$

|  | $\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}}\right)\geq\max\left({Q-x,\delta\eta}\right).$ |  | (68) |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- | ---- |

From Eq. ([46](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#S15.E46 "In Upper bounding the price 𝜋 pays with Alternate Costs ‣ Proof of Theorem ‣ 15 Additional Proofs and Incorporating Item Costs ‣ Adaptive Submodularity: Theory and Applications in Active Learning and Stochastic Optimization")) and the fact that $\pi$ is an$\alpha$-approximate greedy policy by assumption we have that
for all $({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime})\in A_{x}$

|  | $\frac{c(\pi({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}))}{\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{\pi({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)}\leq\frac{\alpha\hat{c}\left({{\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}}\right)}{\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}}\right)}.$ |  | (69) |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- | ---- |

Combining this with Eq. ([68](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#S15.E68 "In Putting it all together ‣ Proof of Theorem ‣ 15 Additional Proofs and Incorporating Item Costs ‣ Adaptive Submodularity: Theory and Applications in Active Learning and Stochastic Optimization")) yields

|  | $\frac{c(\pi({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}))}{\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{\pi({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)}\leq\frac{\alpha\hat{c}\left({{\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}}\right)}{\max\left({Q-x,\delta\eta}\right)}$ |  | (70) |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- | ---- |

for all $({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime})\in A_{x}$.

The expected price $\pi$ pays per unit of expected reward at the instant it
reaches $x$ expected reward is

|  | $\displaystyle\theta(x)$ | $\displaystyle:=$ | $\displaystyle\sum_{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime})\in A_{x}}\mathbb{P}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}\right]\frac{c(\pi({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}))}{\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{\pi({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)}$ |  | (71) |
| --- | ------------------------------ | ------------------------------ | ------------------------------ | --- | ---- |
|  |                                | $\displaystyle\leq$ | $\displaystyle\sum_{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime})\in A_{x}}\mathbb{P}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}\right]\frac{\alpha\hat{c}\left({{\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*}}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}}\right)}{\max\left({Q-x,\delta\eta}\right)}$ |  | (72) |
|  |                                | $\displaystyle=$ | $\displaystyle\frac{\alpha{\hat{c}_{\text{avg}}{(\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*})}}}{\max\left({Q-x,\delta\eta}\right)}$ |  | (73) |

since $\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}:\exists{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime})\in A_{x}\right\}$ partitions
the set of realizations.

Next, we can apply Lemma [47](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#Thmtheorem47 "Lemma 47 ‣ Putting it all together ‣ Proof of Theorem ‣ 15 Additional Proofs and Incorporating Item Costs ‣ Adaptive Submodularity: Theory and Applications in Active Learning and Stochastic Optimization") to bound ${c_{\text{avg}}{(\pi)}}$ as follows.

|  | $\displaystyle{c_{\text{avg}}{(\pi)}}$ | $\displaystyle=$ | $\displaystyle\int_{x=0}^{Q}\theta(x)dx$ |  | (74) |
| --- | ------------------------------ | ------------------------------ | ------------------------------ | --- | ---- |
|  |                                | $\displaystyle\leq$ | $\displaystyle\int_{x=0}^{Q}\frac{\alpha{\hat{c}_{\text{avg}}{(\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*})}}}{\max\left({Q-x,\delta\eta}\right)}dx$ |  | (75) |
|  |                                | $\displaystyle=$ | $\displaystyle\alpha{\hat{c}_{\text{avg}}{(\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*})}}\int_{x=0}^{Q}\frac{1}{\max\left({Q-x,\delta\eta}\right)}dx$ |  | (76) |
|  |                                | $\displaystyle\leq$ | $\displaystyle\alpha{c_{\text{avg}}{(\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*})}}\left({\ln\left({\frac{Q}{\delta\eta}}\right)+1}\right)\int_{x=0}^{Q}\frac{1}{\max\left({Q-x,\delta\eta}\right)}dx$ |  | (77) |
|  |                                | $\displaystyle\leq$ | $\displaystyle\alpha{c_{\text{avg}}{(\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*})}}\left({\ln\left({\frac{Q}{\delta\eta}}\right)+1}\right)^{2}$ |  | (78) |

This completes the proof for general instances (as mentioned, the result for
self–certifying instances
is strictly analogous, merely with different bounds used to relate ${\hat{c}_{\text{avg}}{(\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*})}}$ to ${c_{\text{avg}}{(\pi_{\text{avg}}^{*})}}$),
aside from
proving Lemma [47](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#Thmtheorem47 "Lemma 47 ‣ Putting it all together ‣ Proof of Theorem ‣ 15 Additional Proofs and Incorporating Item Costs ‣ Adaptive Submodularity: Theory and Applications in Active Learning and Stochastic Optimization").
The elegant proof that we present here is
due to [Feldman and Vondrák (2012)](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#bib.bib17).

###### Lemma 47

Suppose $f:2^{E}\times O^{E}\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}$ is adaptive submodular and strongly adaptive
monotone with respect to ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{p\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}}\right)}}$ and there exists $Q$ such that$f(E,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}})=Q$ for all ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}$.
Fix any policy $\pi$ that covers every realization, and let$\theta(x)$ be defined as in the proof
of Theorem [40](https://arxiv.org/html/1003.3967v5#Thmtheorem40 "Theorem 40 ‣ 15.4 The Min-Cost-Cover Objective ‣ 15 Additional Proofs and Incorporating Item Costs ‣ Adaptive Submodularity: Theory and Applications in Active Learning and Stochastic Optimization").
Then

|  | ${c_{\text{avg}}{(\pi)}}\ =\ \int_{x=0}^{Q}\theta(x)dx\mbox{.}$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- |

Proof  Recall

|  | $\theta(x):=\sum_{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime})\in A_{x}}\mathbb{P}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}\right]\frac{c(\pi({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}))}{\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{\pi({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)}$ |  |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- |

We use an alternative charging scheme to compute the expected cost of$\pi$: When the policy observes ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}$ and selects $e=\pi({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})$, we charge it based on the outcome $o={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\phi}}(e)$. Specifically, we charge it proportionally to its gain in
expected reward, as measured by ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\mapsto\mathbb{E}\left[f(\dom({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}})\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}\sim{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}\right]$. Formally, upon observing ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}:={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}\cup\left\{(e,o)\right\}$ after selecting $e=\pi({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}})$, the algorithm is charged

|  | $C_{e}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}):=c(e)\left({\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[f(\dom({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}})\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}\sim{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}^{\prime}}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[f(\dom({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}})\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Phi}}\sim{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}\right]}{\Delta\hskip-0.85358pt\left({{e}\!\mid\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\psi}}}}\right)}}\right).$ |  | (79) |
| --- | ------------------------------ | --- | ---- |
